<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct smooth axisymmetric electrovacuum exteriors that violate the Kerr–Newman Penrose inequality when <i>J</i> is the bare gravitational ADM angular momentum and the electromagnetic fields have only $`O(r^{-2})`$ decay, allowing angularly varying leading tails. The examples have zero total electric and magnetic charge even though both electromagnetic fields are nonzero. They have a connected outermost, outer-area-minimizing future marginally outer trapped boundary and lie strictly on the physical area branch. We also obtain equality examples whose original data admit no Kerr–Newman spacelike realization with the corresponding mass, angular momentum, and charges. These counterexamples do not refute formulations using conserved total angular momentum with its electromagnetic correction or stronger Coulomb asymptotics.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): In the class of smooth axisymmetric source-free electrovacuum exteriors specified in [sec:conventions](#sec:conventions), both of the following occur.* 1. *There are data with $$Q_e=Q_b=0,\qquad A>8\pi\left\lvert J\right\rvert, \qquad m^2<\frac{A}{16\pi}+\frac{4\pi J^2}{A}.$$* 2. *There are data with $$Q_e=Q_b=0,\qquad A>8\pi\left\lvert J\right\rvert, \qquad m^2=\frac{A}{16\pi}+\frac{4\pi J^2}{A},$$ for which no smooth spacelike embedding into the Kerr–Newman spacetime with parameters $(m,J,Q_e,Q_b)$ induces the given metric, second fundamental form, and electromagnetic fields.* *All these examples are maximal, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_gK=0$, on $\Omega=\{x\in\mathbb R^3:\left\lvert x\right\rvert\ge1\}$, with connected boundary $S=\{r=1\}$. Both electromagnetic fields are nonzero.
  - Explanation: Establishes that within the class of smooth axisymmetric source-free electrovacuum exterior initial data sets with maximal slicing on the exterior of the unit ball, there exist configurations with zero total electric and magnetic charges on the strict physical area branch that strictly violate the Kerr-Newman Penrose inequality formulated with bare gravitational ADM angular momentum, as well as configurations achieving exact equality that admit no smooth spacelike embedding into any Kerr-Newman spacetime with the corresponding asymptotic parameters.
- **C2** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-seeds)): The fields $\widetilde E,\widetilde B$ and tensor $\sigma$ are smooth on $\Omega$, invariant under the standard effective $U(1)$ action, and vanish on $\{1\le r\le L\}$. They satisfy $$\label{eq:seed-constraints} \operatorname{div}_\delta\widetilde E =\operatorname{div}_\delta\widetilde B=0,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_\delta\sigma=0,\qquad \operatorname{div}_\delta\sigma =2\mathop{}\!\mathrm dV_\delta(\widetilde E,\widetilde B,\cdot).$$ For every Cartesian multi-index $\alpha$ of order $j\ge0$, $$\label{eq:seed-bounds} \begin{aligned} \|\partial^\alpha\widetilde E\|_\delta +\|\partial^\alpha\widetilde B\|_\delta &\le C_j s r^{-2-j}\mathbf1_{\{r\ge L\}},\\ \|\partial^\alpha\sigma\|_\delta &\le C_j s^2 r^{-3-j}\mathbf1_{\{r\ge L\}}. \end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $\operatorname{div}_\delta\sigma$ is supported in $\{L\le r\le2L\}$, with $$\|\partial^\alpha(\operatorname{div}_\delta\sigma)\|_\delta \le C_j s^2r^{-4-j}\mathbf1_{\{L\le r\le2L\}}.$$
  - Explanation: Constructs explicit smooth axisymmetric Euclidean seed electric and magnetic vector fields and a traceless symmetric covariant tensor vanishing inside radius L that solve the Euclidean divergence-free and electrovacuum momentum constraints and obey uniform inverse-power derivative decay estimates.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-conformal)): For every smooth positive $u$, the data <a href="#eq:conformal-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:conformal-data">[eq:conformal-data]</a> satisfy $$\tau=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_g K=0,\qquad \operatorname{div}_g\mathcal E=\operatorname{div}_g\mathcal B=0, \qquad \operatorname{div}_gK=2(\mathcal E\times_g\mathcal B)^\flat_g.$$ They satisfy the electrovacuum Hamiltonian constraint if and only if $$\label{eq:scalar} -\Delta_\delta u=p u^{-7}+\ell u^{-3}\quad\hbox{on }\Omega,$$ where, writing $t=\cos\theta$, $$\label{eq:coefficients} \begin{aligned} p&=\frac18\|\sigma\|_\delta^2 =\frac{s^4c^4\sin^6\theta}{4r^6},\\ \ell&=\frac14\bigl(\|\widetilde E\|_\delta^2 +\|\widetilde B\|_\delta^2\bigr)\\ &=\frac{s^2}{4r^4} \left[c^2(9t^4-2t^2+1)+(rc')^2(1-t^4)\right]. \end{aligned}$$ The nonnegative coefficients $p,\ell$ are smooth and rotation invariant. For every Cartesian multi-index $\alpha$ of order $j\ge0$, $$\label{eq:source-coefficient-bounds} \|\partial^\alpha p\|\le C_j s^4r^{-6-j}\mathbf1_{\{r\ge L\}},\qquad \|\partial^\alpha\ell\|\le C_j s^2r^{-4-j}\mathbf1_{\{r\ge L\}}.$$
  - Explanation: Demonstrates that conformal rescaling of the Euclidean metric, seed extrinsic curvature tensor, and seed electromagnetic fields preserves maximal slicing, the source-free Maxwell divergence equations, and the electrovacuum momentum constraint, reducing the Hamiltonian constraint to a semilinear scalar elliptic equation on the exterior domain with smooth nonnegative rotation-invariant source coefficients.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-conformal-factor)): There is $L_0>4$, depending only on the fixed cutoff, such that for every $L\ge L_0$ and $s\in[0,1]$, Equation <a href="#eq:scalar" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scalar">[eq:scalar]</a> has a smooth axisymmetric solution $u=u_0+v$ on $\Omega$, where $u_0=1+r^{-1}$, satisfying $$\label{eq:robin} u_r+\tfrac12u=0\quad\text{on }S, \qquad u\longrightarrow1\quad\text{as }r\longrightarrow\infty.$$ It is unique among smooth solutions with $v\ge0$ and $v=O(r^{-1})$. One may take a constant $M=3$, independent of $s,L$, such that $1\le u_0\le u\le M$. Uniformly in these parameters, $$\label{eq:radial-bounds} \begin{aligned} \|v\|+r\|v_r\|&\le Cs^2L^{-2} &&(r\ge1),\\ \|v_{rr}\|&\le Cs^2L^{-2} &&(1\le r\le2). \end{aligned}$$ For every integer $k\ge0$, all Cartesian derivatives satisfy $$\label{eq:all-derivative-bounds} \|D^kv\|\le C_k\frac{s^2}{L}r^{-1-k}, \qquad \|D^k(u-1)\|\le C_k r^{-1-k}.$$ For fixed $L$, $s\mapsto v_s$ is continuous in the sup norm, as are $$s\longmapsto\int_{\mathbb R^3}H_s\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm dx, \qquad s\longmapsto N_s(0)=\frac1{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb R^3}\frac{H_s(x)}{\|x\|}\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm dx,$$ where $H_s=pu_s^{-7}+\ell u_s^{-3}$ is extended by zero inside the unit ball and $N_s$ is its Newton potential. Moreover, $$\label{eq:source-integrals} 0\le\int_{\mathbb R^3}H_s\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm dx\le C\frac{s^2}{L}, \qquad 0\le N_s(0)\le C\frac{s^2}{L^2}.$$ At $s=0$ the solution is exactly $u_0$.
  - Explanation: Proves the existence and uniqueness of a smooth axisymmetric conformal factor solving the scalar Hamiltonian equation with the minimal-surface Robin boundary condition on the unit sphere and asymptotic flatness at infinity, together with uniform pointwise, derivative, and Newton-potential integral bounds and supremum-norm continuity in the amplitude parameter.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-newton)): Suppose $L>4$ and $H\in C(\mathbb R^3)$ obeys $$\|H(y)\|\le a\|y\|^{-4}\mathbf1_{\{\|y\|\ge L\}}.$$ Then $$N_H(x)=\frac1{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb R^3}\frac{H(y)}{\|x-y\|}\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm dy$$ is well defined and $C^1$, satisfies $-\Delta N_H=H$ distributionally, and is smooth and harmonic on $\{\|x\|<L\}$. For $j=0,1$, $$\label{eq:newton-bounds} \|D^jN_H(x)\|\le \begin{cases} C_j aL^{-2-j},& \|x\|\le L/2,\\ C_j a\bigl(r^{-2-j}+L^{-1}r^{-1-j}\bigr),&r=\|x\|\ge L/2. \end{cases}$$ The first estimate holds for every $j\ge0$. The operator $$(\mathcal RH)(x)=N_H(x)+r^{-1}N_H(x/r^2),\qquad x\in\Omega,$$ preserves nonnegativity, satisfies $-\Delta\mathcal RH=H$ distributionally and $(\partial_r+\tfrac12)\mathcal RH=0$ on $S$, and obeys $$\label{eq:robin-operator-bounds} \begin{gathered} \\|\mathcal RH\\|_\infty\le CaL^{-2},\qquad \|\mathcal RH\|+r\|\partial_r\mathcal RH\|\le CaL^{-2},\\ \|\partial_r^2\mathcal RH\|\le CaL^{-2}\quad(1\le r\le2). \end{gathered}$$ The homogeneous harmonic Robin problem on $\Omega$ has no nonzero solution of order $O(r^{-1})$.
  - Explanation: Establishes pointwise and derivative decay estimates for the Euclidean Newton potential and the Kelvin-reflected Robin potential operator associated with continuous source terms supported outside radius L, and proves uniqueness for the homogeneous harmonic Robin problem in the decaying class.
- **C6** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-regularity)): Let $H$ satisfy the hypotheses of [lem:newton](#lem:newton). If $H\in C^j(\mathbb R^3)$, then $N_H\in C^{j+1}(\mathbb R^3)$. If, in addition, $$\|D^iH(y)\|\le a_j\|y\|^{-4-i}\mathbf1_{\{\|y\|\ge L\}} \quad(0\le i\le j),$$ then <a href="#eq:newton-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:newton-bounds">[eq:newton-bounds]</a> holds through order $j+1$, with a constant $C_j a_j$ in place of $C_j a$. Consequently the local gain in regularity and the derivative decay use no first moment of $H$.
  - Explanation: Proves a localized derivative bootstrap for the Newton potential showing that higher differentiability and weighted derivative decay of the source transfer to one higher derivative order of the potential without requiring any finite first-moment assumption.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-geometry)): For all sufficiently large $L$, uniformly for $0\le s\le1$, the data constructed in [sec:construction](#sec:construction), [sec:elliptic](#sec:elliptic) are smooth source-free electrovacuum data on $$\Omega=\{x\in\mathbb R^3:r\ge1\},\qquad S=\{r=1\},$$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_gK=0$. The metric is complete with its boundary included, has one asymptotically flat end, and satisfies $$\label{eq:geometric-falloff} g_{ij}-\delta_{ij}=O_4(r^{-1}),\qquad K_{ij}=O_3(r^{-3}),\qquad \mathcal E^i,\mathcal B^i=O_3(r^{-2}).$$ The standard effective $U(1)$ action preserves all the data and $S$, and $\mu$ and $\|J_{\mathrm{con}}\|_g$ are integrable. With the normal toward the end, $S$ is a future MOTS, and no compact smooth embedded enclosing surface in $\operatorname{int}\Omega$, possibly disconnected, has everywhere nonpositive future outward expansion. Every enclosing cut in the sense of [sec:introduction](#sec:introduction) has area at least $A=\mathop{\mathrm{Area}}_g(S)$. Moreover, for a constant $C_A$ independent of $s,L$, $$\label{eq:area-estimate} 64\pi\le A\le64\pi+C_A\frac{s^2}{L^2}, \qquad A=64\pi+O(s^2/L^2),$$ and $A(s,L)$ is continuous in $s$ for fixed $L$.
  - Explanation: Verifies that the conformally constructed electrovacuum data are smooth, maximal, complete up to the unit-sphere boundary, axisymmetric, and asymptotically flat, with the unit sphere forming a future marginally outer trapped surface that minimizes area among all enclosing cuts and admits no outer trapped enclosing surfaces in the interior.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-spheres)): For $L$ sufficiently large, the coordinate sphere $S_r=\{r=\mathrm{const}\}$ has future outward expansion zero at $r=1$ and strictly positive at every $r>1$. No compact smooth embedded enclosing surface in $\operatorname{int}\Omega$, with any finite number of components, is weakly future outer trapped.
  - Explanation: Shows that for sufficiently large transition radius L, the coordinate spheres have zero future outward expansion on the unit boundary sphere and strictly positive future outward expansion at every larger radius, ruling out any compact smooth embedded weakly future outer trapped enclosing surface in the interior.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-area-minimum)): For the same choices of $L$, every enclosing cut $\Gamma=\partial D$ from the specified class has $\mathop{\mathrm{Area}}_g(\Gamma)\ge\mathop{\mathrm{Area}}_g(S)$. Thus $S$ attains the minimum enclosing area, and <a href="#eq:area-estimate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:area-estimate">[eq:area-estimate]</a> holds.
  - Explanation: Proves by a calibration argument using the pullback of the boundary area form under radial projection that the horizon sphere S achieves the minimum area among all smooth embedded enclosing cuts and obeys the quantitative area sandwich estimate.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-charges)): The constructed data have finite asymptotic quantities $$\label{eq:vanishing-charges} P_{\mathrm{ADM}}=0,\qquad Q_e=Q_b=0,$$ and $$\label{eq:mass} m=2+2N(0)+\frac1{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb R^3}H(x)\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm dx =2+O(s^2/L).$$ In particular $m\ge2$. If $M$ is a uniform upper bound for $u$, then $$\label{eq:mass-lower-bound} m-2\ge\frac{s^2}{3M^3L}.$$ The angular momentum in <a href="#eq:adm-angular" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:adm-angular">[eq:adm-angular]</a> is $$\label{eq:angular-momentum} J=-\frac{4s^2}{15}.$$ For $s>0$, neither electromagnetic field is identically zero.
  - Explanation: Computes the asymptotic ADM linear momentum, electric and magnetic charges, ADM mass, mass lower bound, and bare gravitational ADM angular momentum for the constructed family, and confirms that both electromagnetic fields are nonzero whenever the amplitude parameter is positive.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-corrected-flux)): Let $A_B=h\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm d\phi$, the smooth magnetic potential one-form in [sec:construction](#sec:construction). Then $$\label{eq:corrected-divergence} \operatorname{div}_g\left(K(\cdot,\eta)^{\sharp_g} +2h\mathcal E\right)=0.$$ The electromagnetic boundary contribution on every coordinate sphere is $$\label{eq:electromagnetic-correction} J_{\mathrm{EM}}(r) :=\frac1{4\pi}\int_{S_r}h\,\iota_{\mathcal E}\mathop{}\!\mathrm dV_g =\frac{4s^2c(r)^2}{15}.$$ Consequently $\mathcal J(r):=J_{\mathrm{grav}}(r)+J_{\mathrm{EM}}(r)=0$ for all $r\ge1$, whereas $J_{\mathrm{EM}}(r)\to4s^2/15$ at infinity.
  - Explanation: Establishes a divergence-free current combining the gravitational angular-momentum density with an electromagnetic potential term, showing that the total corrected angular-momentum flux vanishes on every coordinate sphere while the electromagnetic boundary contribution converges to a nonzero value at spatial infinity.
- **C12** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-prop-equality)): For every sufficiently large fixed $L$, there exists $s_*\in(0,1)$ such that $D(s_*,L)=0$. These equality data satisfy the strict branch <a href="#eq:strict-branch" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:strict-branch">[eq:strict-branch]</a> and have no Kerr–Newman realization with the specified asymptotic parameters and induced fields.
  - Explanation: Proves that for every sufficiently large fixed transition radius L, there exists an intermediate amplitude parameter where the Kerr-Newman Penrose defect vanishes on the strict physical area branch, yielding equality initial data that cannot be embedded as a spacelike slice in any Kerr-Newman spacetime with matching asymptotic parameters.

          "source_id": "PM-260.5"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-conformal",
        "exact_quote": "They satisfy the electrovacuum Hamiltonian constraint if and only if $$\\label{eq:scalar}\n -\\Delta_\\delta u=p u^{-7}+\\ell u^{-3}\\quad\\hbox{on }\\Omega,$$",

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-conformal",
          "exact_quote": "For every smooth positive $u$, the data <a href=\"#eq:conformal-data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:conformal-data\">[eq:conformal-data]</a> satisfy $$\\tau=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_g K=0,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{div}_g\\mathcal E=\\operatorname{div}_g\\mathcal B=0,\n \\qquad\n \\operatorname{div}_gK=2(\\mathcal E\\times_g\\mathcal B)^\\flat_g.$$ They satisfy the electrovacuum Hamiltonian constraint if and only if $$\\label{eq:scalar}\n -\\Delta_\\delta u=p u^{-7}+\\ell u^{-3}\\quad\\hbox{on }\\Omega,$$ where, writing $t=\\cos\\theta$, $$\\label{eq:coefficients}\n\\begin{aligned}\n p&=\\frac18|\\sigma|_\\delta^2\n   =\\frac{s^4c^4\\sin^6\\theta}{4r^6},\\\\\n \\ell&=\\frac14\\bigl(|\\widetilde E|_\\delta^2\n                  +|\\widetilde B|_\\delta^2\\bigr)\\\\\n   &=\\frac{s^2}{4r^4}\n      \\left[c^2(9t^4-2t^2+1)+(rc')^2(1-t^4)\\right].\n\\end{aligned}$$ The nonnegative coefficients $p,\\ell$ are smooth and rotation invariant.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-conformal-factor",
        "exact_quote": "It is unique among smooth solutions with $v\\ge0$ and $v=O(r^{-1})$. One may take a constant $M=3$, independent of $s,L$, such that $1\\le u_0\\le u\\le M$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-conformal-factor",
          "exact_quote": "There is $L_0>4$, depending only on the fixed cutoff, such that for every $L\\ge L_0$ and $s\\in[0,1]$, Equation <a href=\"#eq:scalar\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scalar\">[eq:scalar]</a> has a smooth axisymmetric solution $u=u_0+v$ on $\\Omega$, where $u_0=1+r^{-1}$, satisfying $$\\label{eq:robin}\n u_r+\\tfrac12u=0\\quad\\text{on }S,\n \\qquad u\\longrightarrow1\\quad\\text{as }r\\longrightarrow\\infty.$$ It is unique among smooth solutions with $v\\ge0$ and $v=O(r^{-1})$. One may take a constant $M=3$, independent of $s,L$, such that $1\\le u_0\\le u\\le M$. Uniformly in these parameters, $$\\label{eq:radial-bounds}\n \\begin{aligned}\n  |v|+r|v_r|&\\le Cs^2L^{-2} &&(r\\ge1),\\\\\n  |v_{rr}|&\\le Cs^2L^{-2} &&(1\\le r\\le2).\n \\end{aligned}$$ For every integer $k\\ge0$, all Cartesian derivatives satisfy $$\\label{eq:all-derivative-bounds}\n |D^kv|\\le C_k\\frac{s^2}{L}r^{-1-k},\n \\qquad |D^k(u-1)|\\le C_k r^{-1-k}.$$ For fixed $L$, $s\\mapsto v_s$ is continuous in the sup norm, as are $$s\\longmapsto\\int_{\\mathbb R^3}H_s\\,\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm dx,\n \\qquad\n s\\longmapsto N_s(0)=\\frac1{4\\pi}\\int_{\\mathbb R^3}\\frac{H_s(x)}{|x|}\\,\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm dx,$$ where $H_s=pu_s^{-7}+\\ell u_s^{-3}$ is extended by zero inside the unit ball and $N_s$ is its Newton potential. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:source-integrals}\n 0\\le\\int_{\\mathbb R^3}H_s\\,\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm dx\\le C\\frac{s^2}{L},\n \\qquad 0\\le N_s(0)\\le C\\frac{s^2}{L^2}.$$ At $s=0$ the solution is exactly $u_0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is $L_0>4$, depending only on the fixed cutoff, such that for every $L\\ge L_0$ and $s\\in[0,1]$, Equation <a href=\"#eq:scalar\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scalar\">[eq:scalar]</a> has a smooth axisymmetric solution $u=u_0+v$ on $\\Omega$, where $u_0=1+r^{-1}$, satisfying $$\\label{eq:robin}\n u_r+\\tfrac12u=0\\quad\\text{on }S,\n \\qquad u\\longrightarrow1\\quad\\text{as }r\\longrightarrow\\infty.$$ It is unique among smooth solutions with $v\\ge0$ and $v=O(r^{-1})$. The operator $$(\\mathcal RH)(x)=N_H(x)+r^{-1}N_H(x/r^2),\\qquad x\\in\\Omega,$$ preserves nonnegativity, satisfies $-\\Delta\\mathcal RH=H$ distributionally and $(\\partial_r+\\tfrac12)\\mathcal RH=0$ on $S$, and obeys $$\\label{eq:robin-operator-bounds}\n \\begin{gathered}\n \\|\\mathcal RH\\|_\\infty\\le CaL^{-2},\\qquad\n |\\mathcal RH|+r|\\partial_r\\mathcal RH|\\le CaL^{-2},\\\\\n |\\partial_r^2\\mathcal RH|\\le CaL^{-2}\\quad(1\\le r\\le2).\n \\end{gathered}$$ The homogeneous harmonic Robin problem on $\\Omega$ has no nonzero solution of order $O(r^{-1})$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-geometry",
        "exact_quote": "With the normal toward the end, $S$ is a future MOTS, and no compact smooth embedded enclosing surface in $\\operatorname{int}\\Omega$, possibly disconnected, has everywhere nonpositive future outward expansion.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-geometry",
          "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $L$, uniformly for $0\\le s\\le1$, the data constructed in [sec:construction](#sec:construction), [sec:elliptic](#sec:elliptic) are smooth source-free electrovacuum data on $$\\Omega=\\{x\\in\\mathbb R^3:r\\ge1\\},\\qquad S=\\{r=1\\},$$ with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_gK=0$. The metric is complete with its boundary included, has one asymptotically flat end, and satisfies $$\\label{eq:geometric-falloff}\n g_{ij}-\\delta_{ij}=O_4(r^{-1}),\\qquad K_{ij}=O_3(r^{-3}),\\qquad\n \\mathcal E^i,\\mathcal B^i=O_3(r^{-2}).$$ The standard effective $U(1)$ action preserves all the data and $S$, and $\\mu$ and $|J_{\\mathrm{con}}|_g$ are integrable. With the normal toward the end, $S$ is a future MOTS, and no compact smooth embedded enclosing surface in $\\operatorname{int}\\Omega$, possibly disconnected, has everywhere nonpositive future outward expansion. Every enclosing cut in the sense of [sec:introduction](#sec:introduction) has area at least $A=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Area}}_g(S)$. Moreover, for a constant $C_A$ independent of $s,L$, $$\\label{eq:area-estimate}\n 64\\pi\\le A\\le64\\pi+C_A\\frac{s^2}{L^2},\n \\qquad A=64\\pi+O(s^2/L^2),$$ and $A(s,L)$ is continuous in $s$ for fixed $L$.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For all sufficiently large $L$, uniformly for $0\\le s\\le1$, the data constructed in [sec:construction](#sec:construction), [sec:elliptic](#sec:elliptic) are smooth source-free electrovacuum data on $$\\Omega=\\{x\\in\\mathbb R^3:r\\ge1\\},\\qquad S=\\{r=1\\},$$ with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_gK=0$. The metric is complete with its boundary included, has one asymptotically flat end, and satisfies $$\\label{eq:geometric-falloff}\n g_{ij}-\\delta_{ij}=O_4(r^{-1}),\\qquad K_{ij}=O_3(r^{-3}),\\qquad\n \\mathcal E^i,\\mathcal B^i=O_3(r^{-2}).$$ The standard effective $U(1)$ action preserves all the data and $S$, and $\\mu$ and $|J_{\\mathrm{con}}|_g$ are integrable. With the normal toward the end, $S$ is a future MOTS, and no compact smooth embedded enclosing surface in $\\operatorname{int}\\Omega$, possibly disconnected, has everywhere nonpositive future outward expansion. Every enclosing cut in the sense of [sec:introduction](#sec:introduction) has area at least $A=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Area}}_g(S)$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Kerr-Newman Penrose inequality** ([source](#eq:kn-inequality)): The Kerr–Newman mass formula suggests the Kerr–Newman Penrose inequality $$\label{eq:kn-inequality} m^2\ge \frac{A}{16\pi}+\frac{Q^2}{2} +\frac{\pi(Q^4+4J^2)}{A}, \qquad A\ge4\pi\sqrt{Q^4+4J^2},$$ where $A$ is a suitable enclosing area, $m$ is mass, $Q$ is total charge, and $J$ is angular momentum.
- **Riemannian cross product** ([source](#sec:conventions)): On an oriented Riemannian three-manifold $(\Omega,g)$, the cross product is defined by $$(X\times_gY)^\flat(Z)=\mathop{}\!\mathrm dV_g(X,Y,Z).$$
- **Source-free electrovacuum constraint equations** ([source](#sec:conventions)): We use the source-free electrovacuum constraint equations $$\label{eq:constraints} \begin{split} R_g+\tau^2-\left\lvert K\right\rvert_g^2 &=2\bigl(\left\lvert\mathcal E\right\rvert_g^2+\left\lvert\mathcal B\right\rvert_g^2\bigr),\\ \operatorname{div}_g(K-\tau g) &=2(\mathcal E\times_g\mathcal B)^\flat,\\ \operatorname{div}_g\mathcal E &=\operatorname{div}_g\mathcal B=0. \end{split}$$
- **Total constraint densities** ([source](#sec:conventions)): Thus the total constraint densities are $$\mu=\frac{\left\lvert\mathcal E\right\rvert_g^2+\left\lvert\mathcal B\right\rvert_g^2}{8\pi}, \qquad J_{\mathrm{con}} =\frac{(\mathcal E\times_g\mathcal B)^\flat}{4\pi}.$$
- **Asymptotic ADM and electromagnetic quantities** ([source](#eq:falloff)): The asymptotic quantities are $$\begin{aligned} E_{\mathrm{ADM}} &=\frac1{16\pi}\lim_{R\to\infty} \int_{S_R}(\partial_jg_{ij}-\partial_ig_{jj}) n_\delta^i\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm dA_\delta,\label{eq:adm-energy}\\ (P_{\mathrm{ADM}})_i &=\frac1{8\pi}\lim_{R\to\infty} \int_{S_R}(K_{ij}-\tau g_{ij})n_\delta^j\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm dA_\delta, \label{eq:adm-momentum}\\ J&=\frac1{8\pi}\lim_{R\to\infty} \int_{S_R}(K_{ij}-\tau g_{ij})\nu_R^i\eta^j\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm dA_g, \label{eq:adm-angular}\\ Q_e&=\frac1{4\pi}\lim_{R\to\infty} \int_{S_R}g(\mathcal E,\nu_R)\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm dA_g, \qquad Q_b=\frac1{4\pi}\lim_{R\to\infty} \int_{S_R}g(\mathcal B,\nu_R)\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm dA_g. \label{eq:charges-definition} \end{aligned}$$
- **Future outward null expansion and future MOTS** ([source](#sec:conventions)): For a two-sided surface with unit normal $\nu$ pointing toward the end, put $$\label{eq:expansion} H_\Sigma=\operatorname{div}_\Sigma\nu, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_\Sigma K=(g^{ij}-\nu^i\nu^j)K_{ij}, \qquad \theta_+(\Sigma)=H_\Sigma+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_\Sigma K.$$
- **Enclosing cut and minimum enclosing area** ([source](#sec:conventions)): An *enclosing cut* is the compact intrinsic boundary $\Gamma=\partial D$ of a connected smooth codimension-zero submanifold $D\subset\Omega$, closed as a subset of $\Omega$, whose interior lies in $\operatorname{int}\Omega$ and which contains the entire sufficiently distant part of the end.
- **Axisymmetric electrovacuum seed potentials, fields, and tensor** ([source](#eq:seeds)): Define the scalar potentials, vector fields, and symmetric covariant tensor by $$\label{eq:seeds} \begin{aligned} f&=sc(r)\sin^2\theta,& h&=sc(r)\sin^2\theta\cos\theta,\\ \iota_{\widetilde E}\mathop{}\!\mathrm dV_\delta&=\mathop{}\!\mathrm d(f\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm d\phi),& \iota_{\widetilde B}\mathop{}\!\mathrm dV_\delta&=\mathop{}\!\mathrm d(h\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm d\phi),\\ \sigma&=-\frac{s^2c(r)^2\sin^4\theta}{r^2} (\mathop{}\!\mathrm dr\otimes\mathop{}\!\mathrm d\phi+\mathop{}\!\mathrm d\phi\otimes\mathop{}\!\mathrm dr). \end{aligned}$$
- **Conformal physical electrovacuum data** ([source](#eq:conformal-data)): For a positive function $u$, define the physical data by $$\label{eq:conformal-data} g=u^4\delta,\qquad K=u^{-2}\sigma,\qquad \mathcal E=u^{-6}\widetilde E,\qquad \mathcal B=u^{-6}\widetilde B.$$
- **Robin Kelvin-reflection potential operator** ([source](#stmt-lem-newton)): The operator $$(\mathcal RH)(x)=N_H(x)+r^{-1}N_H(x/r^2),\qquad x\in\Omega,$$ preserves nonnegativity, satisfies $-\Delta\mathcal RH=H$ distributionally and $(\partial_r+\tfrac12)\mathcal RH=0$ on $S$, and obeys $$\label{eq:robin-operator-bounds} \begin{gathered} \\|\mathcal RH\\|_\infty\le CaL^{-2},\qquad \|\mathcal RH\|+r\|\partial_r\mathcal RH\|\le CaL^{-2},\\ \|\partial_r^2\mathcal RH\|\le CaL^{-2}\quad(1\le r\le2). \end{gathered}$$
- **Radial-projection calibration two-form** ([source](#eq:calibration-comass)): Define the smooth closed two-form $$\beta=\pi^*\bigl(u(1,\omega)^4\mathop{}\!\mathrm dA_{\mathbb S^2}\bigr).$$
- **Conserved electromagnetic angular-momentum correction** ([source](#stmt-prop-corrected-flux)): The electromagnetic boundary contribution on every coordinate sphere is $$\label{eq:electromagnetic-correction} J_{\mathrm{EM}}(r) :=\frac1{4\pi}\int_{S_r}h\,\iota_{\mathcal E}\mathop{}\!\mathrm dV_g =\frac{4s^2c(r)^2}{15}.$$
- **Kerr-Newman Penrose defect functional** ([source](#pm-h-12)): Set $$\label{eq:defect} D(s,L)=m(s,L)^2-\frac{A(s,L)}{16\pi} -\frac{4\pi J(s,L)^2}{A(s,L)}.$$

| Q11 | m^2-\left\lvert J\right\rvert | > | $0$ | Equality parameter s_* in the open unit interval for sufficiently large fixed transition radius L. |
| Q12 | P_{\mathrm{ADM}}, Q_e, Q_b | = | $0$ | All constructed electrovacuum initial data sets in the two-parameter family. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-seeds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-seeds): Explicit construction in spherical and Cartesian coordinates using exact azimuthal two-forms and radial cutoff functions, followed by direct tensor-divergence and homogeneity derivative estimates. -> Smooth rotation-invariant Euclidean seed fields and traceless tensor vanishing inside radius L with exact divergence and momentum identities.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-conformal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conformal): Conformal transformation of the volume form, Levi-Civita connection difference tensor, extrinsic curvature divergence, and scalar curvature to reduce the electrovacuum constraint system to a single scalar equation. -> Equivalence between the full electrovacuum constraint equations and the semilinear scalar elliptic Hamiltonian equation with nonnegative source coefficients.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-newton) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-newton): Dyadic domain splitting of the Euclidean Newton kernel integral, three-dimensional Kelvin reflection across the unit sphere, and maximum-principle uniqueness for harmonic extensions. -> Pointwise and gradient decay bounds for the Newton potential and Kelvin-reflected Robin operator, plus uniqueness for the homogeneous harmonic Robin problem.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-regularity): Localized integration by parts with a smooth cutoff scaled to the evaluation radius to bootstrap higher Cartesian derivatives of the Newton potential without first-moment assumptions. -> Higher-order regularity and uniform weighted derivative decay estimates for the Newton potential.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-prop-conformal-factor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-regularity-2): Banach contraction mapping on the cone of nonnegative bounded functions using the Robin Kelvin-reflection operator for large transition radius L, combined with inductive derivative bootstrapping and Lipschitz parameter continuity. -> Unique smooth axisymmetric conformal factor solving the Lichnerowicz scalar equation with uniform derivative and integral bounds.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-positive-spheres) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-spheres): Radial expansion weight analysis on the near-horizon collar and outer region, combined with a maximum-radius barrier tangency argument and topological detour surgery around edged surface components. -> Vanishing outward expansion on the unit boundary sphere, strict positivity on outer coordinate spheres, and absence of weakly future outer trapped enclosing surfaces in the interior.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-area-minimum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-area-minimum): Calibration argument pulling back the horizon area form via radial projection, bounding its comass by one using monotonicity of r times u squared, and applying Stokes theorem on truncated enclosing domains. -> Global area-minimization of the unit boundary sphere among all smooth embedded enclosing cuts and two-sided horizon area bounds.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-geometry): Synthesis of conformal metric equivalence, curve-length completeness, asymptotic derivative decay, integrability of constraint densities, and the horizon barrier and calibration lemmas. -> Verification that the constructed family satisfies all geometric, topological, asymptotic, future MOTS, outermostness, and minimum-enclosing-area requirements.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-prop-charges) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-charges): Direct evaluation of asymptotic sphere flux integrals using exactness of the electromagnetic flux two-forms, Gauss divergence flux identities for the Newton potential and its Kelvin reflection, and exact angular integration of the seed tensor. -> Exact values and asymptotic bounds for ADM linear momentum, electric and magnetic charges, ADM mass, and bare gravitational ADM angular momentum.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-prop-corrected-flux) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-corrected-flux): Application of Cartan magic formula to the invariant magnetic potential one-form and the Killing equation for the azimuthal generator to construct a divergence-free corrected angular-momentum current. -> Conservation and vanishing of total gravitational-plus-electromagnetic angular-momentum flux across all coordinate spheres, with nonvanishing asymptotic electromagnetic contribution.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-prop-equality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-equality): Intermediate value theorem applied to the continuous Penrose defect functional between small positive amplitude and unit amplitude, combined with subextremal zero-charge Kerr uniqueness of the vanishing Maxwell tensor. -> Existence of an intermediate amplitude root achieving exact Penrose equality on the strict physical branch without any smooth spacelike Kerr-Newman realization.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-equality-2): Assembly of the geometric validity proposition, asymptotic flux calculations, large-radius negative defect limit at unit amplitude, and intermediate-amplitude equality obstruction. -> Complete proof of Theorem 1 establishing both strict violation and non-realizable equality for the bare-angular-momentum Kerr-Newman Penrose inequality.

Its positive value at a nonzero small parameter and its negative value at $s=1$ yield a root $s_*\\in(0,1)$ by the intermediate value theorem.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.5"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "eq:subextremality",
          "exact_quote": "m^2-\\left\\lvert J\\right\\rvert=\\frac{(A-8\\pi\\left\\lvert J\\right\\rvert)^2}{16\\pi A}>0.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "R11",
      "inputs": [
        "C4",
        "C9",
        "C10"
      ],
      "method": "Intermediate value theorem applied to the continuous Penrose defect functional between small positive amplitude and unit amplitude, combined with subextremal zero-charge Kerr uniqueness of the vanishing Maxwell tensor.",
      "output": "Existence of an intermediate amplitude root achieving exact Penrose equality on the strict physical branch without any smooth spacelike Kerr-Newman realization.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-equality"
    },

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **INV-01**: Enforce the source-free electrovacuum constraints in three spatial dimensions with $G=c=1$ and zero cosmological constant, maximal slicing $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_gK=0$ on $\Omega=\{x\in\mathbb R^3:\left\lvert x\right\rvert\ge1\}$, the falloff conditions for the metric, extrinsic curvature, and fields $\mathcal E,\mathcal B=O(r^{-2})$, and vanishing linear momentum $P_{\mathrm{ADM}}=0$ with bare gravitational ADM angular momentum $J$.
- **INV-02**: Separate the axisymmetric seed fields $\widetilde E,\widetilde B$ and traceless tensor $\sigma$ from the unit sphere so that they vanish on $\{1\le r\le L\}$ with $L\ge L_0$ and $L_0>4$, ensuring unique solvability of the conformal scalar equation for $u=u_0+v$ in the nonnegative cone $v\ge0$ with Robin boundary condition $\partial_ru+u/2=0$ on $S$.
- **INV-03**: Maintain zero future outward expansion $\theta_+(S)=0$ on the boundary sphere $S=\{r=1\}$, strictly positive outward expansion on every coordinate sphere $r>1$, and calibration comass at most $1$ so that $S$ is outermost and attains the minimum enclosing area $A=\mathop{\mathrm{Area}}_g(S)$ across all enclosing cuts.
- **INV-04**: Distinguish the bare gravitational angular-momentum flux from the electromagnetic boundary contribution whose conserved sum $\mathcal J(r):=J_{\mathrm{grav}}(r)+J_{\mathrm{EM}}(r)=0$ vanishes for all $r\ge1$ while $J_{\mathrm{EM}}(r)\to4s^2/15$ at infinity.
- **INV-05**: Keep the counterexample and equality conclusions scoped to the bare gravitational ADM flux $J$ on the strict physical area branch with $Q=0$, where strict inequality violation $D(1,L)<0$ occurs at $s=1$ and nondegenerate equality $D(s_*,L)=0$ at $s_*\in(0,1)$ admits no smooth spacelike embedding into the Kerr–Newman spacetime with parameters $(m,J,Q_e,Q_b)$.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **AU-01**: Use the constructed counterexamples to claim a violation of Kerr–Newman Penrose inequality formulations defined with conserved total angular momentum including the electromagnetic boundary correction. -> Confuses bare gravitational ADM angular-momentum flux at infinity with the conserved total angular momentum $\mathcal J(r):=J_{\mathrm{grav}}(r)+J_{\mathrm{EM}}(r)=0$.
- **AU-02**: Apply the violation conclusions to electrovacuum exteriors obeying stronger Coulomb asymptotic expansions rather than $\mathcal E,\mathcal B=O(r^{-2})$ field decay. -> Extrapolates an obstruction driven by angularly varying $\mathcal E,\mathcal B=O(r^{-2})$ radial electromagnetic tails to regimes where those leading tails are excluded by stronger falloff.
- **AU-03**: Infer a smooth spacelike embedding into the Kerr–Newman spacetime with parameters $(m,J,Q_e,Q_b)$ from exact equality $D(s_*,L)=0$ on the strict physical area branch with $Q=0$. -> A zero-charge Kerr–Newman spacetime has vanishing Maxwell tensor $F=0$ on every spacelike slice, contradicting the nonzero constructed electromagnetic fields.
- **AU-04**: Apply the existence, positive sphere expansion, or strict violation estimates for transition radii $L$ below the uniform threshold $L_0>4$ or without separating the seed support from the unit sphere. -> The contraction mapping bound for $T_s$, the Robin reflection barrier positivity, and the negative defect sign $D(1,L)<0$ require $L$ to be sufficiently large.
- **AU-05**: Restrict the outer-area-minimization check to axisymmetric radial graphs or discard portions of an enclosing cut $\Gamma=\partial D$ that coincide with the horizon boundary $S$. -> Omits admissible disconnected or non-symmetric competitors or undercounts intrinsic boundary area.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. Not applicable to a finite constructive or algorithmic certificate of the paper results. Proposition 4 constructs the conformal factor perturbation via an infinite-dimensional Banach contraction mapping on the cone of nonnegative continuous functions on the exterior manifold with unquantified cutoff constants and threshold radius; Lemma 9 proves area minimization over all smooth enclosing cuts by a continuum Stokes calibration; Proposition 12 obtains the equality parameter nonconstructively by the intermediate value theorem. These continuum PDE and variational arguments do not specify a finite input encoding or finite decision procedure, and arithmetic evaluation of the asymptotic formulas alone cannot certify the paper hypotheses.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L01** (counterexample_scope): The counterexamples apply to the formulation using bare gravitational ADM angular momentum $J$ and $\mathcal E,\mathcal B=O(r^{-2})$ electromagnetic decay; they do not refute formulations retaining the electromagnetic boundary term $J_{\mathrm{EM}}(r)\to4s^2/15$ in the conserved total angular momentum $\mathcal J(r):=J_{\mathrm{grav}}(r)+J_{\mathrm{EM}}(r)=0$ or imposing stronger Coulomb asymptotics. Blocked use: ['Claiming a violation of the Kerr–Newman Penrose inequality for conserved total angular momentum including the electromagnetic boundary correction.', 'Claiming a violation under stronger Coulomb asymptotic expansions where electromagnetic fields decay as $O(r^{-3})$ rather than retaining angularly varying $r^{-2}$ tails.']
- **L02** (hypothesis): The exterior class and constructed counterexamples are formulated in three spatial dimensions with $G=c=1$, zero cosmological constant, maximal slicing $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_gK=0$, a single asymptotically flat end on $\Omega=\{x\in\mathbb R^3:\left\lvert x\right\rvert\ge1\}$ with connected boundary $S=\{r=1\}$, and a smooth effective $U(1)$ symmetry. Blocked use: ['Extrapolating the counterexample family directly to nonzero cosmological constant or non-axisymmetric geometries without separate analysis.', 'Applying the conformal reduction formulas without maximal slicing $\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_gK=0$.']
- **L03** (hypothesis): The construction requires the source-free electrovacuum constraint equations, asymptotic derivative bounds of order $O_k(r^{-a})$ with $K=O(r^{-3})$ and $\mathcal E,\mathcal B=O(r^{-2})$, integrable constraint densities $\mu$ and $\left\lvert J_{\mathrm{con}}\right\rvert_g$, and vanishing ADM linear momentum $P_{\mathrm{ADM}}=0$ with $E_{\mathrm{ADM}}>0$ so that $m=E_{\mathrm{ADM}}$. Blocked use: ['Setting mass $m=E_{\\mathrm{ADM}}$ without verifying vanishing linear momentum $P_{\\mathrm{ADM}}=0$.', 'Dropping integrability of $\\mu$ or $\\left\\lvert J_{\\mathrm{con}}\\right\\rvert_g$ or the derivative decay bounds $O_k(r^{-a})$ when evaluating asymptotic fluxes.']
- **L04** (hypothesis): The horizon boundary $S$ is a future marginally outer trapped surface ($\theta_+(S)=0$) that is outermost in the sense that no compact smooth embedded enclosing surface in $\operatorname{int}\Omega$, possibly disconnected, has $\theta_+\le0$ everywhere; no condition on the inward expansion is imposed. Blocked use: ['Assuming a two-sided minimal or time-symmetric apparent horizon condition on $S$ when only $\\theta_+(S)=0$ is imposed.', 'Inferring bounds on the inward null expansion from the future outward expansion condition.']
- **L05** (edge_case): An enclosing cut $\Gamma=\partial D$ is the full compact intrinsic boundary of a closed connected smooth submanifold $D\subset\Omega$ containing the distant end, counting any portion coinciding with $S$ and allowing disconnected, non-symmetric, or untrapped cuts. Blocked use: ['Discarding boundary portions of $\\Gamma$ that coincide with the inner boundary $S$ when testing $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Area}}_g(\\Gamma)\\ge\\mathop{\\mathrm{Area}}_g(S)$.', 'Restricting enclosing-area comparisons only to connected radial graphs or axisymmetric surfaces.']
- **L06** (counterexample_scope): The counterexamples have vanishing total asymptotic electric and magnetic charges $Q_e=Q_b=0$ even though both electromagnetic fields $\mathcal E,\mathcal B$ are nonzero for $s>0$, supported by divergence-free angularly varying $r^{-2}$ radial tails without curl-free or stationarity assumptions. Blocked use: ['Assuming $Q_e=Q_b=0$ implies that the electric and magnetic fields decay as $O(r^{-3})$ or vanish asymptotically.', 'Imposing electrostatic or stationary curl-free field equations on the electrovacuum initial data.']
- **L07** (hypothesis): Existence, uniqueness in the nonnegative cone $\mathcal C=\{v\in C_b(\Omega):v\ge0\}$, positive outward sphere expansion, and area-minimization require the transition radius $L$ to satisfy $L\ge L_0$ for a threshold $L_0>4$ depending on the fixed cutoff so that the seeds vanish on $\{1\le r\le L\}$. Blocked use: ['Applying the contraction mapping or positive sphere expansion estimates when the condition $L>4$ fails and sources are not separated from the unit sphere $S=\\{r=1\\}$.', 'Assuming the negative defect inequality $D(1,L)<0$ holds at arbitrary finite $L$ below the uniform threshold.']
- **L08** (edge_case): All family members satisfy $A\ge64\pi$ and lie strictly on the physical area branch where the Kerr–Newman Penrose right-hand side increases with enclosing area $A$ and $Q=0$. Blocked use: ['Attributing the Penrose inequality violation to the unphysical small-area branch where the lower area bound fails.']
- **L09** (nonconstructive): The conformal factor perturbation $v\in\mathcal C$ is obtained by an infinite-dimensional Banach contraction mapping on $\mathcal C=\{v\in C_b(\Omega):v\ge0\}$ with cutoff constants $C,C_j$ and threshold $L_0>4$, and the equality parameter $s_*\in(0,1)$ satisfying $D(s_*,L)=0$ is obtained nonconstructively via the intermediate value theorem. Blocked use: ['Treating the analytical existence proof as a finite closed-form formula for the conformal factor $u=u_0+v$ or the equality root $s_*\\in(0,1)$.', 'Claiming an explicit numerical value for the threshold $L_0>4$ or the root $s_*\\in(0,1)$ without fixing and bounding all cutoff constants $C,C_j$.']
- **L10** (approximation): The noncompact source $H$ of decay $r^{-4}$ need not have a finite first moment, so the mass expansion with $\int H=O(s^2/L)$ and $N(0)=O(s^2/L^2)$ is established by direct flux identities and Kelvin reflection rather than a standard multipole expansion. Blocked use: ['Assuming a standard dipole or finite first-moment multipole expansion for the ordinary Newton potential at infinity.', 'Dropping the $\\int H=O(s^2/L)$ mass integral or $N(0)=O(s^2/L^2)$ term when evaluating the defect $D(s,L)$ at finite $L$.']
- **L11** (formal_scope): At the equality root $s_*\in(0,1)$, the obstruction to a smooth spacelike embedding into the Kerr–Newman spacetime with parameters $(m,J,Q_e,Q_b)$ holds for every spacelike slice—including nonconstant time graphs and slices extending to the future horizon or bifurcation sphere—because $Q_e=Q_b=0$ forces the Kerr–Newman Maxwell tensor to satisfy $F=0$ while the constructed fields $\mathcal E,\mathcal B$ are nonzero. Blocked use: ['Attempting to realize the $Q_e=Q_b=0$ equality initial data as a nonconstant time slice of the Kerr–Newman spacetime.', 'Using the equality example to claim a geometric embedding obstruction of the intrinsic metric $g$ alone without reference to the nonzero induced Maxwell fields $\\mathcal E,\\mathcal B$.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct smooth axisymmetric electrovacuum exteriors that violate the Kerr–Newman Penrose inequality when <i>J</i> is the bare gravitational ADM angular momentum and the electromagnetic fields have only $`O(r^{-2})`$ decay, allowing angularly varying leading tails. The examples have zero total electric and magnetic charge even though both electromagnetic fields are nonzero. They have a connected outermost, outer-area-minimizing future marginally outer trapped boundary and lie strictly on the physical area branch. We also obtain equality examples whose original data admit no Kerr–Newman spacelike realization with the corresponding mass, angular momentum, and charges. These counterexamples do not refute formulations using conserved total angular momentum with its electromagnetic correction or stronger Coulomb asymptotics.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
